QLoRA backpropagates gradients through a frozen, 4-bit quantized pretrained language model into Low Rank Adapters (LoRA). Our best model family, which we name Guanaco, outperforms all previous openly released models on the Vicuna benchmark, reaching 99.3% of the performance level of ChatGPT while only requiring 24 hours of finetuning on a single GPU. QLoRA introduces a number of innovations to save memory without sacrificing performance: (a) 4-bit NormalFloat (NF4), a new data type that is information theoretically optimal for normally distributed weights (b) Double Quantization to reduce the average memory footprint by quantizing the quantization constants, and (c) Paged Optimizers to manage memory spikes. We use QLoRA to finetune more than 1,000 models, providing a detailed analysis of instruction following and chatbot performance across 8 instruction datasets, multiple model types (LLaMA, T5), and model scales that would be infeasible to run with regular finetuning (e.g. 33B and 65B parameter models). Our results show that QLoRA finetuning on a small high-quality dataset leads to state-of-the-art results, even when using smaller models than the previous SoTA. We provide a detailed analysis of chatbot performance based on both human and GPT-4 evaluations showing that GPT-4 evaluations are a cheap and reasonable alternative to human evaluation. Furthermore, we find that current chatbot benchmarks are not trustworthy to accurately evaluate the performance levels of chatbots. We release all of our models and code, including CUDA kernels for 4-bit training.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eLicense and Intended Use\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-license-and-intended-use\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: License and Intended Use\" href=\"#license-and-intended-use\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eWe release the resources associated with QLoRA finetuning in this repository under MIT license.\nIn addition, we release the Guanaco model family for base LLaMA model sizes of 7B, 13B, 33B, and 65B. \u003ccode\u003eBitsandbytesConfig\u003c/code\u003e (\u003ca href=\"https://huggingface.co/docs/transformers/main_classes/quantization#transformers.BitsAndBytesConfig\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003esee HF \u003ccode\u003ebnb_4bit_compute_dtype\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eNested quantization is activated through Note that there are two supported quantization datatypes \u003ccode\u003efp4\u003c/code\u003e (four bit float) and \u003ccode\u003enf4\u003c/code\u003e (normal four bit float). We include script for comparisons relative to ChatGPT with scores out of 10 as well as \"pairwise comparisons\" with three class labeling (win, loose, or tie). These are found in the \u003ccode\u003eeval\u003c/code\u003e folder.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eTo facilitate the replication of our evaluation and future work in this area, we release GPT-4 and human ratings of our systems. These are found under \u003ccode\u003eeval/ratings-human\u003c/code\u003e and \u003ccode\u003eeval/ratings-gpt4\u003c/code\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eMore details can be found at \u003ccode\u003eeval/EVAL_README.md\u003c/code\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eKnown Issues and Limitations\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-known-issues-and-limitations\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Known Issues and Limitations\" href=\"#known-issues-and-limitations\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eHere a list of known issues and bugs. If your issue is not reported here, please open a new issue and describe the problem.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003col dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e4-bit inference is slow. Currently, our 4-bit inference implementation is not yet integrated with the 4-bit matrix multiplication\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eResuming a LoRA training run with the Trainer currently not supported by HF.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eCurrently, using \u003ccode\u003ebnb_4bit_compute_type='fp16'\u003c/code\u003e can lead to instabilities. For 7B LLaMA, only 80% of finetuning runs complete without error. We have solutions, but they are not integrated yet into bitsandbytes.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eMake sure that \u003ccode\u003etokenizer.bos_token_id = 1\u003c/code\u003e to avoid generation issues.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eIf you get an this \u003ca href=\"https://github.com/artidoro/qlora/issues/82\" data-hovercard-type=\"issue\" data-hovercard-url=\"/artidoro/qlora/issues/82/hovercard\"\u003eissue\u003c/a\u003e (\"illegal memory access\") then you should use a newer HF LLaMA conversion or downgrade your PyTorch version.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eProblems with adding new tokens outlined in #214. Embeddings need to be updated and stored/reloaded if you are adding new tokens.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ol\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class="markdown-body entry-content container-lg" itemprop="text"><div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h1 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">QLoRA: Efficient Finetuning of Quantized LLMs</h1><a id="user-content-qlora-efficient-finetuning-of-quantized-llms" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: QLoRA: Efficient Finetuning of Quantized LLMs" href="#qlora-efficient-finetuning-of-quantized-llms"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">| <a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.14314" rel="nofollow">Paper</a> | <a href="https://huggingface.co/timdettmers" rel="nofollow">Adapter Weights</a> | <a href="https://huggingface.co/spaces/uwnlp/guanaco-playground-tgi" rel="nofollow">Demo</a> |</p>
<p dir="auto">This repo supports the paper "QLoRA: Efficient Finetuning of Quantized LLMs", an effort to democratize access to LLM research.</p>
<p dir="auto">QLoRA uses <a href="https://github.com/TimDettmers/bitsandbytes">bitsandbytes</a> for quantization and is integrated with Hugging Face's <a href="https://github.com/huggingface/peft">PEFT</a> and <a href="https://github.com/huggingface/transformers/">transformers</a> libraries. QLoRA was developed by members of the <a href="https://twitter.com/uwnlp?s=20" rel="nofollow">University of Washington's UW NLP group</a>.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Updates</h2><a id="user-content-updates" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Updates" href="#updates"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<ul dir="auto">
<li>7/19/2023 - Added LLaMA 2 example script and updated version requirements</li>
<li>7/18/2023 - Fixed non-frozen embeddings when adding new tokens</li>
</ul>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Overview</h2><a id="user-content-overview" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Overview" href="#overview"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">We present QLoRA, an efficient finetuning approach that reduces memory usage enough to finetune a 65B parameter model on a single 48GB GPU while preserving full 16-bit finetuning task performance. QLoRA backpropagates gradients through a frozen, 4-bit quantized pretrained language model into Low Rank Adapters (LoRA). Our best model family, which we name Guanaco, outperforms all previous openly released models on the Vicuna benchmark, reaching 99.3% of the performance level of ChatGPT while only requiring 24 hours of finetuning on a single GPU. QLoRA introduces a number of innovations to save memory without sacrificing performance: (a) 4-bit NormalFloat (NF4), a new data type that is information theoretically optimal for normally distributed weights (b) Double Quantization to reduce the average memory footprint by quantizing the quantization constants, and (c) Paged Optimizers to manage memory spikes. We use QLoRA to finetune more than 1,000 models, providing a detailed analysis of instruction following and chatbot performance across 8 instruction datasets, multiple model types (LLaMA, T5), and model scales that would be infeasible to run with regular finetuning (e.g. 33B and 65B parameter models). Our results show that QLoRA finetuning on a small high-quality dataset leads to state-of-the-art results, even when using smaller models than the previous SoTA. We provide a detailed analysis of chatbot performance based on both human and GPT-4 evaluations showing that GPT-4 evaluations are a cheap and reasonable alternative to human evaluation. Furthermore, we find that current chatbot benchmarks are not trustworthy to accurately evaluate the performance levels of chatbots. We release all of our models and code, including CUDA kernels for 4-bit training.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">License and Intended Use</h2><a id="user-content-license-and-intended-use" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: License and Intended Use" href="#license-and-intended-use"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">We release the resources associated with QLoRA finetuning in this repository under MIT license.
In addition, we release the Guanaco model family for base LLaMA model sizes of 7B, 13B, 33B, and 65B. These models are intended for purposes in line with the LLaMA license and require access to the LLaMA models.</p>

<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Installation</h2><a id="user-content-installation" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Installation" href="#installation"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">To load models in 4bits with transformers and bitsandbytes, you have to install accelerate and transformers from source and make sure you have the latest version of the bitsandbytes library. After installing PyTorch (follow instructions <a href="https://pytorch.org/get-started/locally/" rel="nofollow">here</a>), you can achieve the above with the following command:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="pip install -U -r requirements.txt"><pre>pip install -U -r requirements.txt</pre></div>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Getting Started</h2><a id="user-content-getting-started" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Getting Started" href="#getting-started"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">The <code>qlora.py</code> code is a starting point for finetuning and inference on various datasets.
Basic command for finetuning a baseline model on the Alpaca dataset:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="python qlora.py --model_name_or_path &lt;path_or_name&gt;"><pre>python qlora.py --model_name_or_path <span class="pl-k">&lt;</span>path_or_name<span class="pl-k">&gt;</span></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">For models larger than 13B, we recommend adjusting the learning rate:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="python qlora.py –learning_rate 0.0001 --model_name_or_path &lt;path_or_name&gt;"><pre>python qlora.py –learning_rate 0.0001 --model_name_or_path <span class="pl-k">&lt;</span>path_or_name<span class="pl-k">&gt;</span></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">To replicate our Guanaco models see below.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Tutorials and Demonstrations</h3><a id="user-content-tutorials-and-demonstrations" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Tutorials and Demonstrations" href="#tutorials-and-demonstrations"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">Here is <a href="https://huggingface.co/blog/4bit-transformers-bitsandbytes" rel="nofollow">a blog</a> discussing 4-bit quantization, QLoRA, and how they are integrated in transformers.</p>

<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Quantization</h3><a id="user-content-quantization" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Quantization" href="#quantization"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">Quantization parameters are controlled from the <code>BitsandbytesConfig</code> (<a href="https://huggingface.co/docs/transformers/main_classes/quantization#transformers.BitsAndBytesConfig" rel="nofollow">see HF documenation</a>) as follows:</p>
<ul dir="auto">
<li>Loading in 4 bits is activated through <code>load_in_4bit</code></li>
<li>The datatype used for the linear layer computations with <code>bnb_4bit_compute_dtype</code></li>
<li>Nested quantization is activated through <code>bnb_4bit_use_double_quant</code></li>
<li>The datatype used for qunatization is specified with <code>bnb_4bit_quant_type</code>. Note that there are two supported quantization datatypes <code>fp4</code> (four bit float) and <code>nf4</code> (normal four bit float). The latter is theoretically optimal for normally distributed weights and we recommend using <code>nf4</code>.</li>
</ul>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="    model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(
        model_name_or_path='/name/or/path/to/your/model',
        load_in_4bit=True,
        device_map='auto',
        max_memory=max_memory,
        torch_dtype=torch.bfloat16,
        quantization_config=BitsAndBytesConfig(
            load_in_4bit=True,
            bnb_4bit_compute_dtype=torch.bfloat16,
            bnb_4bit_use_double_quant=True,
            bnb_4bit_quant_type='nf4'
        ),
    )"><pre>    <span class="pl-s1">model</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-v">AutoModelForCausalLM</span>.<span class="pl-c1">from_pretrained</span>(
        <span class="pl-s1">model_name_or_path</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s">'/name/or/path/to/your/model'</span>,
        <span class="pl-s1">load_in_4bit</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">True</span>,
        <span class="pl-s1">device_map</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s">'auto'</span>,
        <span class="pl-s1">max_memory</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s1">max_memory</span>,
        <span class="pl-s1">torch_dtype</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s1">torch</span>.<span class="pl-c1">bfloat16</span>,
        <span class="pl-s1">quantization_config</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-en">BitsAndBytesConfig</span>(
            <span class="pl-s1">load_in_4bit</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">True</span>,
            <span class="pl-s1">bnb_4bit_compute_dtype</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s1">torch</span>.<span class="pl-c1">bfloat16</span>,
            <span class="pl-s1">bnb_4bit_use_double_quant</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">True</span>,
            <span class="pl-s1">bnb_4bit_quant_type</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s">'nf4'</span>
        ),
    )</pre></div>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Paged Optimizer</h3><a id="user-content-paged-optimizer" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Paged Optimizer" href="#paged-optimizer"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">You can access the paged optimizer with the argument <code>--optim paged_adamw_32bit</code></p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Guanaco Finetuning</h3><a id="user-content-guanaco-finetuning" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Guanaco Finetuning" href="#guanaco-finetuning"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">You can select <code>--dataset oasst1</code> to load the OpenAssistant dataset that was used to train Guanaco. You can also find it on HF at <a href="https://huggingface.co/datasets/timdettmers/openassistant-guanaco" rel="nofollow">timdettmers/openassistant-guanaco</a>.</p>
<p dir="auto">We include scripts to reproduce the hyperparameters of Guanaco model training for various sizes at <code>./scripts/finetune_guanaco*.sh</code>. Make sure to adjust <code>per_device_train_batch_size</code> and <code>gradient_accumulation_steps</code> so that their product is 16 and training fits on your GPUs.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Using Local Datasets</h3><a id="user-content-using-local-datasets" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Using Local Datasets" href="#using-local-datasets"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">You can specify the path to your dataset using the <code>--dataset</code> argument. If the <code>--dataset_format</code> argument is not set, it will default to the Alpaca format. Here are a few examples:</p>
<ul dir="auto">
<li>Training with an <em>alpaca</em> format dataset:
<div class="highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="python qlora.py --dataset=&quot;path/to/your/dataset&quot;"><pre>python qlora.py --dataset=<span class="pl-s"><span class="pl-pds">"</span>path/to/your/dataset<span class="pl-pds">"</span></span></pre></div>
</li>
<li>Training with a <em>self-instruct</em> format dataset:
<div class="highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="python qlora.py --dataset=&quot;path/to/your/dataset&quot; --dataset_format=&quot;self-instruct&quot;"><pre>python qlora.py --dataset=<span class="pl-s"><span class="pl-pds">"</span>path/to/your/dataset<span class="pl-pds">"</span></span> --dataset_format=<span class="pl-s"><span class="pl-pds">"</span>self-instruct<span class="pl-pds">"</span></span></pre></div>
</li>
</ul>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Multi GPU</h3><a id="user-content-multi-gpu" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Multi GPU" href="#multi-gpu"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">Multi GPU training and inference work out-of-the-box with Hugging Face's Accelerate. Note that the <code>per_device_train_batch_size</code> and <code>per_device_eval_batch_size</code> arguments are  global batch sizes unlike what their name suggest.</p>
<p dir="auto">When loading a model for training or inference on multiple GPUs you should pass something like the following to <code>AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained()</code>:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="device_map = &quot;auto&quot;
max_memory = {i: '46000MB' for i in range(torch.cuda.device_count())}"><pre><span class="pl-s1">device_map</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s">"auto"</span>
<span class="pl-s1">max_memory</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> {<span class="pl-s1">i</span>: <span class="pl-s">'46000MB'</span> <span class="pl-k">for</span> <span class="pl-s1">i</span> <span class="pl-c1">in</span> <span class="pl-en">range</span>(<span class="pl-s1">torch</span>.<span class="pl-c1">cuda</span>.<span class="pl-c1">device_count</span>())}</pre></div>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Sample Outputs</h2><a id="user-content-sample-outputs" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Sample Outputs" href="#sample-outputs"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">We provide generations for the models described in the paper for both OA and Vicuna queries in the <code>eval/generations</code> folder. These are intended to foster further research on model evaluation and analysis.</p>
<p dir="auto">Can you distinguish ChatGPT from Guanaco? Give it a try!
You can access <a href="https://colab.research.google.com/drive/1kK6xasHiav9nhiRUJjPMZb4fAED4qRHb?usp=sharing" rel="nofollow">the model response Colab here</a> comparing ChatGPT and Guanaco 65B on Vicuna prompts.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Evaluation</h2><a id="user-content-evaluation" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Evaluation" href="#evaluation"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">We include scripts adapted from the FastChat repo to automatically evaluate model generations using GPT-4. We include script for comparisons relative to ChatGPT with scores out of 10 as well as "pairwise comparisons" with three class labeling (win, loose, or tie). These are found in the <code>eval</code> folder.</p>
<p dir="auto">To facilitate the replication of our evaluation and future work in this area, we release GPT-4 and human ratings of our systems. These are found under <code>eval/ratings-human</code> and <code>eval/ratings-gpt4</code>.</p>
<p dir="auto">More details can be found at <code>eval/EVAL_README.md</code>.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Known Issues and Limitations</h2><a id="user-content-known-issues-and-limitations" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Known Issues and Limitations" href="#known-issues-and-limitations"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">Here a list of known issues and bugs. If your issue is not reported here, please open a new issue and describe the problem.</p>
<ol dir="auto">
<li>4-bit inference is slow. Currently, our 4-bit inference implementation is not yet integrated with the 4-bit matrix multiplication</li>
<li>Resuming a LoRA training run with the Trainer currently not supported by HF.</li>
<li>Currently, using <code>bnb_4bit_compute_type='fp16'</code> can lead to instabilities. For 7B LLaMA, only 80% of finetuning runs complete without error. We have solutions, but they are not integrated yet into bitsandbytes.</li>
<li>Make sure that <code>tokenizer.bos_token_id = 1</code> to avoid generation issues.</li>
<li>If you get an this <a href="https://github.com/artidoro/qlora/issues/82" data-hovercard-type="issue" data-hovercard-url="/artidoro/qlora/issues/82/hovercard">issue</a> ("illegal memory access") then you should use a newer HF LLaMA conversion or downgrade your PyTorch version.</li>
<li>Problems with adding new tokens outlined in #214. Embeddings need to be updated and stored/reloaded if you are adding new tokens.</li>
</ol>
